L1489: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1490: ## 15.1. LUNA 3
L1491: ## 15.1.1. Clinical Protocol Synopsis for LUNA 3, (PRN1008-018), Version 4, July 24, 2023
L1492: ## PROTOCOL TITLE
L1493: A Phase 3, Multicenter, Randomized, Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Parallel-Group Study with an Open-Label Extension to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of Oral Rilzabrutinib (PRN1008) in Adults and Adolescents with Persistent or Chronic Immune Thrombocytopenia (ITP)
L1494: ## PROTOCOL NUMBER
L1495: PRN1008-018 (EFC17093)
L1496: ## SPONSOR
L1497: Principia Biopharma Inc.
L1498: NDA 219685
WAYRILZ (rilzabrutinib)
L1499: ## STUDY MEDICATION
L1500: The investigational drug product is rilzabrutinib (PRN1008/SAR444671).
L1501: ## PHASE
L1502: This is a Phase 3 pivotal study.
L1503: ## STUDY POPULATION
L1504: Participants with refractory or relapsed ITP of &gt;3 months duration (age 18 years and above) or with &gt;6 months duration (age \ge 12 to &lt;18 years; see Appendix 10.2, Appendix 10.3, and Appendix 10.7 for country-specific criteria).
L1505: ## DURATION OF STUDY PARTICIPATION
L1506: For each participant, the study will last up to 60 weeks from the start of the screening period to the End of Study (EOS) visit. This includes screening (up to 4 weeks) through a 12 to 24-week blinded treatment period followed by a 28-week open-label period. Followed by a 4-week postdose follow-up. For adult participants, the maximum duration of the LTE period will be 12 months from the date of the last adult participant to enter the LTE.
L1507: For pediatric participants, the maximum duration of the LTE period will be 12 months from the date of the last pediatric participant to enter the LTE.
L1508: ## NUMBER OF STUDY SITES
L1509: Approximately 150 sites worldwide.
L1510: ## OBJECTIVES
L1511: ## Primary Efficacy Objective (Blinded Treatment Period)
L1512: - To demonstrate the efficacy of rilzabrutinib versus placebo in participants with refractory/relapsed ITP, based on the durability of platelet response during the last 12 weeks of the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy
L1513: ## Key Secondary Efficacy Objectives (Blinded Treatment Period)
L1514: - To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib versus placebo on the number of weeks with platelet count \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline, over the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy.
- To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib versus placebo on the number of weeks with platelet counts \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline over the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy.
- To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib versus placebo on the time to first platelet count of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline.
- To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib versus placebo on the proportion of participants requiring rescue therapy.
- To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib versus placebo on the change from baseline on Item 10 of the ITP-PAQ (i.e., physical fatigue) in adult participants (\ge 18 years) at Week 13.
L1515: See Appendix 10.7 for EU (European Economic Area (EEA) countries) and UK-specific requirements.
L1516: ## Other Secondary Objectives
L1517: ## Efficacy Objectives
L1518: - To evaluate the stability of platelet response of rilzabrutinib Safety Objectives.
- To evaluate the safety and tolerability of rilzabrutinib in pediatric participants (&lt;18 years) and in adult participants (\ge 18 years) with refractory/relapsed ITP.
L1519: ## Pharmacokinetic Objectives
L1520: - To characterize the PK of rilzabrutinib in pediatric participants (&lt;18 years) and in adult participants (\ge 18 years) with refractory/relapsed ITP.
L1521: ## Quality of Life Objectives
L1522: - To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib on the general and disease-specific quality-of-life (QOL) of adult participants (\ge 18 years) with refractory/relapsed ITP.
- To evaluate the effect of rilzabrutinib on disease-specific QOL in pediatric participants with refractory/relapsed ITP.
L1523: Refer to Appendix 10.7 for EU (EEA countries) specific age ranges.
L1524: ## INVESTIGATIONAL PLAN
L1525: ## Study Design
L1526: This is a global, randomized, parallel-group, double-blind, multicenter clinical study in participants with primary ITP who had a response to either intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIg) or corticosteroid (CS) that was not sustained.
L1527: After providing informed consent, participants will enter a 28-day screening period. Upon completion of the screening period, participants who satisfy all the inclusion criteria and none of the exclusion criteria of this protocol will be randomized in a 2:1 allocation ratio to one of two study arms: rilzabrutinib or placebo.
L1528: Randomization will be carried out separately for the two age groups. For the adult group, stratified permuted block randomization will be implemented; for the pediatric group, dynamic randomization algorithm (minimization) will be implemented. The factors used for stratification (for adult participants) or balance (for pediatric participants) are splenectomy status (yes/no), and by severity of thrombocytopenia (Inclusion Criteria #3 platelet counts &lt;15,000/\mu\text{L} or \ge 15,000/\mu\text{L}).
L1529: After randomization, participants will start a blinded treatment period of up to 25 weeks followed by an open-label period of 28 weeks during which all participants will receive rilzabrutinib, and then a 4-week safety follow-up period or long-term extension.
L1530: At the end of 12 weeks of treatment, participants will be assessed for achieving a platelet response defined as:
L1531: - platelet count of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR a platelet count between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline at any time during the first 12 weeks and
- absence of rescue medication in the 4 weeks prior to the elevated platelet count that meets platelet response criteria.
L1532: Figure 1 (of the protocol) depicts the decision process for assessing response. The baseline value of platelet count is defined as the average of all the participant's Predose platelet counts (Screening and Study Day 1).
L1533: - Participants who respond will continue the blinded treatment period for a total of 24 weeks before entering the open-label period.
- Participants who do not respond (including participants who receive rescue medication after 8 weeks of treatment) may discontinue from the study or enter the 28-week open-label period at the end of Week 12, receiving treatment with 400 mg BID of rilzabrutinib. The initial study medication assignment will remain blinded.
L1534: Concomitant ITP medications (an oral CS and/or a thrombopoietin receptor agonist [TPO-RA]) will be permitted in both treatment arms and must be maintained at stable doses from 14 days before Study Day 1 until the last dose of study medication. Reductions in the doses of concomitant ITP medications will be permitted for associated safety concerns only.
L1535: The use of rescue medications (one of IVIg, high-dose CSs, platelet infusion, or anti-D immunoglobulin infusion) intended to increase platelet counts or prevent bleeding when platelet counts are less than 20,000/\mu\text{L}, or for bleeding or wet purpura, will be allowed.
L1536: After completing the open-label period, participants who demonstrate a platelet response defined as platelet counts \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} or \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline at \ge 50\% of the visits without receiving rescue therapy while on treatment during the last 8 weeks of the open-label period, will be allowed to enter the LTE.
L1537: Participant(s) may continue in the LTE until:
L1538: - The participant is no longer responding (platelet counts &lt;30,000/\mu\text{L} or less than 20,000/\mu\text{L} above baseline on two consecutive visits)
- The drug is no longer being developed by the Sponsor for ITP
- The program is stopped for safety reasons or
- The drug becomes commercially available in the participant's country.
L1539: ## **Safety Measures Due to Coronavirus (COVID-19) Pandemic**
L1540: Due to the COVID-19 pandemic, safety measures have been implemented to ensure continued supply of study medication and safety monitoring for participants. These measures are described in the "Guidelines to Sites for Delayed Participant Visits or Missed Visits due to travel restrictions and any foreseeable impacts of COVID-19." When the COVID-19 pandemic resolves, the measures will be repealed back to the previous state as government rules and benefit/risk assessment allow.
L1541: ## **STUDY DRUG AND DOSING REGIMEN**
L1542: Participants will receive one 400 mg tablet of rilzabrutinib or placebo BID in the double-blind portion of the study. Participants will receive one 400 mg tablet of rilzabrutinib BID in the open-label portion of the study and the LTE.
L1543: Tablets should be taken with (~8oz/250mL) of water and may be taken with or without food.
L1544: ## **PLANNED NUMBER OF PARTICIPANTS**
L1545: - Up to 30 pediatric participants aged 12 to &lt;18 years; pediatric participants 10 to &lt;12 years will be enrolled only in EEA countries; see Appendix 10.2, Appendix 10.3, and Appendix 10.7 for country-specific age ranges).
- Approximately 194 participants aged 18 years or older.
L1546: ## **INDIVIDUAL PARTICIPANT STOPPING RULES**
L1547: - Life-threatening or CTCAE Grade 4 rilzabrutinib-related TEAE except for adverse events (AEs) related to the disease under study or other underlying medical conditions
- Serious allergic reaction to rilzabrutinib or placebo, including anaphylactic reaction
- Pregnancy
- Any medical condition or personal circumstance that, in the opinion of the Investigator, exposes the participant to risk if the participant continues with rilzabrutinib or placebo or that prevents the participant's adherence to the protocol
- Human immunodeficiency virus (HIV)/acquired immune deficiency syndrome (AIDS), viral hepatitis (B and C) infection occurring during the study
- Violation of protocol inclusion or exclusion criteria, that in the opinion of the Sponsor, would significantly compromise data interpretation
- Discontinuation of study intervention for abnormal liver tests is required by the Investigator when a participant meets one of the conditions outlined in Appendix 15 or in the presence of
L1548: abnormal liver chemistries not meeting protocol-specified stopping rules if the Investigator believes that it is in best interest of the participant.
L1549: ## STUDY STOPPING RULES
L1550: Study stopping rules will include, however not be limited to:
L1551: - More than one rilzabrutinib-related death
- Two or more life-threatening or CTCAE Grade 4 rilzabrutinib-related TEAEs except for AEs related to the disease under study (lack of efficacy)
- The Sponsor elects to stop the study.
L1552: ## INCLUSION CRITERIA
L1553: Participants may be included in the study if ALL of the following criteria are met:
L1554: - Participants will be male and female with primary ITP with duration of &gt;6 months in pediatric participants aged 12 to &lt;18 years (pediatric participants aged 10 to &lt;12 years will be enrolled in the EU [EEA countries] only; refer to Appendix 10.2, Appendix 10.3, and Appendix 10.7 for country-specific requirements) and duration of &gt;3 months in adults aged \ge 18 years.
- Participants who had a response (achievement of platelet count \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L}) to IVIg/anti-D or CSs that was not sustained and who have documented intolerance, insufficient response, or any contraindication to any appropriate courses of standard of care ITP therapy
- An average of 2 platelet counts at least 5 days apart of &lt;30,000/\mu\text{L} during the screening period, and no single platelet count &gt;35,000/\mu\text{L}, within 14 days prior to the first dose of study drug.Pediatric participants must additionally be determined to need treatment for ITP as per clinical assessment by the Investigator (see Appendix 10.7 for EU [EEA countries] specific requirements).
- Adequate hematologic, hepatic, and renal function (absolute neutrophil count \ge 1.5 \times 10^9/\text{L}, aspartate aminotransferase/alanine aminotransferase \le 1.5 \times \text{ULN}, albumin \ge 3 \text{ g/dL}, total bilirubin \le 1.5 \times \text{ULN} [unless the participant has documented Gilbert syndrome], glomerular filtration rate &gt;50 [Cockcroft and Gault method]).
- Hemoglobin &gt;9 \text{ g/dL} within 1 week prior to Study Day 1.
L1555: 6. All contraceptive use by men and women should be consistent with local regulations regarding the methods of contraception for those participating in clinical studies.

a. Male participants
Male participants are eligible to participate if they agree to the following during the intervention period and for at least 13 weeks after the last administration of study intervention:

- Refrain from donating or cryopreserving sperm plus either:Be abstinent from heterosexual intercourse as their preferred and usual lifestyle (abstinent on a long term and persistent basis) and agree to remain abstinent
- ORMust agree to use contraception/barrier as detailed below.
- A male condom: the participant should also be advised of the benefit for a female partner to use a highly effective method of contraception (as described in Appendix 13 Contraceptive and barrier guidance of the protocol) as a condom may break or leak when having sexual intercourse with a woman of childbearing potential (WOCBP) who is not currently pregnant.
b. Female participants
A female participant is eligible to participate if she is not pregnant or breastfeeding, and one of the following conditions applies:

- Is a woman of nonchildbearing potential (WONCBP) as defined in Appendix 13 of the protocol.
- OR
- Is a WOCBP and agrees to use a contraceptive method that is highly effective (with a failure rate of &lt;1% per year), with low user dependency, as described in Appendix 13 of the protocol, during the study intervention period (to be effective before starting the intervention) and for at least 4 weeks after the last administration of study intervention AND agrees not to donate or cryopreserve eggs (ova, oocytes) for the purpose of reproduction during this period.
- A WOCBP must have a negative highly sensitive pregnancy test (serum) as required by local regulations) within 3 days before the first administration of study intervention.If a urine test cannot be confirmed as negative (e.g., an ambiguous result), a serum pregnancy test is required. In such cases, the participant must be excluded from participation if the serum pregnancy result is positive.
7. Participants must be able to provide written informed consent or informed assent with corresponding informed consent obtained from the participant's guardian and agree to the schedule of assessments.
L1556: ## EXCLUSION CRITERIA
L1557: Participants will be excluded from the study if any of the following criteria are met:
L1558: - Participants with secondary ITP
- Pregnant or lactating women
- Electrocardiogram findings for participants:Aged \ge 10 and &lt;16 years: QTcF &gt;449 msec (males) or &gt;457 msec (females)
- Aged \ge 16 and &lt;18 years: QTcF &gt;450 msec (males) or &gt;460 msec (females)
- Aged \ge 18 years, of QTcF &gt;450 msec (males) or &gt;470 msec (females), poorly controlled atrial fibrillation (i.e., symptomatic participants or a ventricular rate above 100 beats/min on ECG), or other clinically significant abnormalities
- History (within 5 years of Study Day 1) or current, active malignancy requiring or likely to require chemotherapeutic or surgical treatment during the study, with the exception of non-melanoma skin cancer
- Transfusion with blood, blood products, plasmapheresis, or use of any other rescue medications with intent to increase platelet count within 14 days before Study Day 1
- Change in CS and/or TPO-RA dose within 14 days prior to Study Day 1 (more than 10% variation from current doses)
- Immunosuppressant drugs other than CSs within 5 times the elimination half-life of the drug or 14 days of Study Day 1, whichever is longer
- Treatment with rituximab or splenectomy within the 3 months prior to Study Day 1Participants treated with rituximab will have normal B-cell counts prior to enrollment.
- Ongoing need for the use of proton pump inhibitor drugs such as omeprazole and esomeprazole (it is acceptable to change participant to histamine 2 receptor blocking drugs prior to Study Day 1)
- Use of known strong-to-moderate inducers or inhibitors of cytochrome P450 (CYP) 3A within 14 days or 5 half-lives (whichever is longer) of Study Day 1 and until the end of the active treatment period
- Planned or concomitant use of any anticoagulants and platelet aggregation inhibiting drugs such as aspirin (except for low dose aspirin up to 100 mg per day), nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drugs, and/or thienopyridines within 14 days of Study Day 1 and until the end of the active treatment period
- Has received any investigational drug within the 30 days before receiving the first dose of study medication, or at least 5 times elimination half-life of the drug (whichever is longer); participant should not be using an investigational device at the time of dosingParticipants who previously received treatment with BTK inhibitors (except rilzabrutinib) within 30 days before the first dose of study drug are not eligible
- Participants who previously received rilzabrutinib at any time are not eligible
- Current drug or alcohol abuse
- Refractory nausea and vomiting, malabsorption, external biliary shunt, significant bowel resection, or any other condition that would preclude adequate study drug absorption
- History of solid organ transplant
L1559: 16. Positive at Screening for HIV, hepatitis B virus (HBV) (surface and core antibodies unrelated to vaccination), or hepatitis C virus (anti-HCV antibody confirmed with Hep C RNA)

- Participants who are hepatitis B virus surface antigen (HBsAg) positive will not be eligible.
- Participants who are HBsAg negative and hepatitis B core antigen antibody (HBcAb) positive will be tested for HBV surface antibody (HBsAb) and HBV DNA. If HBV DNA is negative and HBsAb titer is \ge 100 IU/L, participants may be enrolled. Monthly HBV DNA monitoring will be required while on treatment and for 6 months after the last dose of the study drug. Positive HBV DNA results will be managed appropriately as per local standard of care.
- Participants who are HBcAb positive and HBsAg negative with HBsAb titer &lt;100 IU/L or negative, are not eligible.
17. Positive QuantiFERON®-TB Gold, or QuantiFERON®-TB Gold Plus (QFT Plus) at Screening unless all of the following 3 conditions are true:

- Chest X-ray does not show evidence suggestive of active tuberculosis (TB) disease
- There are no clinical signs and symptoms of pulmonary and/or extrapulmonary TB disease
- Documented receipt of one of the following prophylactic treatment regimens:Oral daily Isoniazid for 6 months or
- Oral daily Rifampin for 4 months or
- Isoniazid and Rifapentine weekly for 3 months (3HP)
On a case-by-case basis, after discussion and approval by the Sponsor, a local TB test that is negative and is considered equivalent to 1 of the above tests may be used for eligibility. For example, if a QuantiFERON®-TB Gold, or QuantiFERON-TB Gold Plus (QFT Plus) is indeterminate for any reason and a local blood test or T-Spot® TB test is negative, the participant may be enrolled using the local result upon approval of the Sponsor.

18. History of recurring (2 or more) serious infections requiring intravenous antibiotic, antivirals or antifungals therapy within the last 3 months before Study Day 1 or active serious or moderate infection ongoing on the day of randomization

19. Myelodysplastic syndrome

20. Live vaccine within 28 days prior to Study Day 1 or plan to receive one during the study

21. Planned surgery in the time frame of the dosing period

22. Any other clinically significant disease, condition, known allergy to any of the study medications, their analogues, or excipients in the various formulations of any agent (please see Section 6.1.1 for information on excipients), or medical history that, in the opinion of the Investigator or Sponsor's medical monitor, would interfere with participant safety, study evaluations, and/or study procedures

23. Positive severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus-2 (SARS-CoV-2) molecular test (if COVID-19 testing required per local guidelines to be determined for each site)

24. The COVID-19 vaccine within 14 days prior to Study Day 1 or planned during the last 12 weeks of the blinded treatment period
L1560: ## RANDOMIZATION PROCEDURE
L1561: Participants will be randomized in a 2:1 ratio to either active rilzabrutinib or placebo. The study is designed so that randomization will be carried out separately for the two age groups. For the adult group, stratified permuted block randomization will be implemented; for the pediatric group, dynamic randomization algorithm (minimization) will be implemented. The factors used for stratification or balance are splenectomy status (yes/no), and by severity of thrombocytopenia (Inclusion Criteria 3 platelet counts &lt;15,000/\mu\text{L} or \ge 15,000/\mu\text{L}).
L1562: ## STUDY ASSESSMENTS
L1563: ## Clinical Assessments Will Include:
L1564: - Physical examination; assessment of vital signs, height, and weight; 12-lead ECG, collection of medical history, collection of concomitant medication information, assessment of bleeding with IBLs and/or other bleeding scales, QOL questionnaires, collection of AEs.
L1565: ## Laboratory Assessments Will Include:
L1566: - Hepatitis B and C, HIV, pregnancy test, follicle-stimulating hormone, urinalysis, ABO and Rh blood tests, hemolysis panel (Coombs and haptoglobin), mean platelet volume, serum chemistry, hematology, platelet counts, prothrombin time /international normalized ratio, thrombopoietin level, PK sampling, T-lymphocytes/B-lymphocytes/natural killers count, Immunoglobulin levels, activated partial thromboplastin time, fibrinogen, and BTK occupancy (at select sites).
L1567: ## SAFETY AND TOLERABILITY ASSESSMENTS
L1568: Safety and tolerability will be assessed by collection of frequency, severity, and causal relationship of TEAEs.
L1569: ## Data Safety Monitoring Board
L1570: An independent Data Safety Monitoring Board will review unblinded participant safety data.
L1571: ## ENDPOINTS
L1572: ## Primary Efficacy Endpoint (Blinded Treatment Period)
L1573: - Durable platelet response defined as the proportion of participants able to achieve platelet counts at or above 50,000/\mu\text{L} for \ge two-thirds of at least 8 nonmissing weekly scheduled platelet measurements during the last 12 weeks of the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy, provided that at least 2 nonmissing weekly scheduled platelet measurements are at or above 50,000/\mu\text{L} during the last 6 weeks of the 24-week blinded treatment period; see Appendix 10.7 for country-specific definition of durable platelet response (EU [EEA countries] and UK).
L1574: ## Key Secondary Efficacy Endpoints (Blinded Treatment Period)
L1575: - Number of weeks with platelet count \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline over the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy
- Number of weeks with platelet counts \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline over the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy
- Time to first platelet count of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and doubled from baseline
- Proportion of participants requiring rescue therapy during the 24-week blinded treatment period
- Change from baseline on Item 10 of the ITP-PAQ (i.e., physical fatigue) in adult participants (\ge 18 years) at Week 13
L1576: ## SAMPLE SIZE
L1577: The adult sample size chosen for this study was selected to achieve enrollment of 129 adult participants (\ge 18 years) on rilzabrutinib and 65 adult participants on placebo. The pediatric sample size of up to 30 participants (20 participants on rilzabrutinib and 10 participants on placebo) was determined based on clinical practice and is adequate to descriptively describe the safety and efficacy in pediatric participants. With a sample size of 20 pediatric participants on rilzabrutinib the maximum width of an exact 90% CI on response rate would be 40%.
L1578: A sample size of approximately 194 (129 versus 65 adult participants in the rilzabrutinib versus placebo arms, respectively) will provide 95% power to detect a 20% difference in response rates as defined in the primary endpoint (Section 3.2.1) between the 2 arms (25% versus 5%, in the rilzabrutinib versus placebo arms, respectively), using the Fisher's Exact test with a 0.05 two-sided significance level.

L1584: AE = adverse event; aPTT = activated partial thromboplastin time; BTK = Bruton's tyrosine kinase; D = day; ECG = electrocardiogram; EQ-5D-5L = EuroQoL-5 Dimension 5 Level; FSH = follicle-stimulating hormone; HBV = hepatitis B virus; HBsAg = hepatitis B surface antigen; Hep = hepatitis; HIV = human immunodeficiency virus; IBLS = ITP Bleeding Scale; IgG = immunoglobulin G; INR = international normalized level; IMP = investigational medicinal product; ITP = immune thrombocytopenia; ITP-PAQ = ITP- Patient Assessment Questionnaire; ITP- KIT = Kids' ITP Tools; PGIC = Patient Global Impression of Change; PGIS = Patient Global Impression of Severity; PGIS-Fatigue = Patient Global Impression of [Fatigue] Severity; PK = pharmacokinetics; PT = Prothrombin time; QOL = Quality of Life; Rh = rhesus factor; SARS-CoV-2 = severe acute respiratory syndrome-related coronavirus-2; SD = study day; TB = tuberculosis; T/B/NK = T-lymphocytes/B-lymphocytes/natural killers; TPO = Thrombopoietin; WK = Week.
L1585: a Participants may be re-screened once. The screening period can be up to -31 days of Study Day 1.

b At the Week 13 visit review previous platelet counts for response during the first 12 weeks. If the participant met the criteria for response continue with the Week 13 visit assessments, the participant should continue in the blinded part. If the participant did not meet the criteria for response, complete the Week 25 visit assessments and the participant may start the open label period or discontinue from the study. Platelet response is defined as a) a platelet count of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR a platelet count of between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline at any time during the first 12 weeks and b) absence of rescue medication in the 4 weeks prior to the latest elevated platelet count that meets platelet response criteria.) Participants who receive rescue medication during the last 12 weeks also may join the open-label part or discontinue from the study. See Figure 1 for decision tree for assessing response at Week 13 and for calculation of baseline platelet count.

c For pediatric participants (see Appendix 10.7 for EU [EEA countries] age ranges) height is recorded at Screening, Week 13, Week 25, Week 37 and Week 53. For participants aged 18 years and older height is recorded at Screening only.

d Full physical examination at Screening, 4-week follow-up and early termination; abbreviated physical examination at all other visits. Medical history should include vaccine history. See Appendix 10.6 for Italy-specific requirements.

e ECG for pediatric participants (see Appendix 10.7 for EU [EEA countries] age ranges) will be performed predose and 2 hours postdose (\pm 15 mins) at the Week 1 and Week 25 visits.

f Urinalysis: pH, specific gravity, protein, glucose, ketones, bilirubin, blood, nitrates, urobilinogen, and leukocytes.

g Participants enrolled with positive HBV core antibody test result and HBsAb titer \ge 100 IU/L will have HBV DNA monitored monthly while on treatment and for 6 months after treatment has ended.

h For women of childbearing potential only. Serum pregnancy test must be performed at Screening; a urine pregnancy test must be performed at all other study timepoints.

i To confirm postmenopausal status for women who are not surgically sterile and of reproductive potential.

j Historical lab results may be used for ABO and Rh Blood Type.

k Serum chemistry will include the following: aspartate aminotransferase (AST); alanine aminotransferase (ALT); total, direct, and indirect bilirubin levels; alkaline phosphatase (ALP); albumin; creatinine; urea; total protein; sodium; chloride; calcium; phosphate; potassium; and glucose (random). Creatine phosphokinase (CPK) and estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) is to be performed at Screening only.

l Hematology will include the following: hemoglobin, hematocrit, erythrocyte count (red blood cell [RBC] count), thrombocyte count (platelets), mean platelet volume, leukocyte count (white blood cell [WBC] count) with differential in absolute counts (including neutrophils, eosinophils, basophils, lymphocytes, and monocytes). Local laboratories will be used for platelet analysis required at ALL visits (ie, Clinic Visits and Weekly Lab Visits in between Clinic Visits).
The central laboratory will be used for the analyses required at Clinic Visits including hematology, differential, and reticulocytes. The results of hematology, differential and reticulocytes obtained at local laboratories will be used only if the central lab results of Clinic Visits are missing due to any reason. Central platelet counts assessed at Clinic Visits will be used if local platelet results are missing for any reason.

m Coagulation will include: PT/INR, thrombin time, activated partial thromboplastin time (aPTT), and fibrinogen level.

n Hemolysis panel: consisting of Coombs test, haptoglobin levels.

o PK samples for participants aged 18 years or older will be collected predose (within 1.5 hours predose) and 2 hours postdose (\pm 15 minutes) at the Week 1 visit. PK samples for pediatric participants (see Appendix 10.7 for EU [EEA countries] age ranges) will be taken Predose (within 1.5 hours predose) and postdose at the following timepoints: 0.5 hours (\pm 5 mins), 2 hours (\pm 5 mins) and 4 hours (\pm 15 mins), 6 hours (\pm 15 mins) at the Week 1 visit. At the Week 13 visit a sample will be taken Predose only. At these visits, participants will wait to take their morning dose until they complete the blood draw of pre-dose sample.

p BTK Occupancy samples for participants aged 18 years or older will be collected Predose (within 1.5 hours predose) and 2 hours postdose (\pm 15 mins) at the Week 1 visit. BTK Occupancy samples for pediatric participants (see Appendix 10.7 for EU [EEA countries] age ranges) will be taken Predose (within 1.5 hours predose) and postdose at the following timepoints: 0.5 hours (\pm 5 mins), 2 hours (\pm 5 mins) and 4 hours (\pm 15 mins), 6 hours (\pm 15 mins) at the Week 1 visit. At the Week 13 visit a sample will be taken Predose only. At these visits, participants will wait to take their morning dose until they complete the blood draw of pre-dose sample.

L1604: ## 15.1.2. Summary of Protocol Amendments
L1605: ## Amended Clinical Trial Protocol 02 (February 19, 2021)
L1606: This was the first protocol amendment to Clinical Study Protocol PRN1008-018. Changes were primarily clarifications in nature.
L1607: ## Amended Clinical Trial Protocol 02 (July 21, 2021)
L1608: The following major clinical changes were included in the protocol amendment:
L1609: - Stratification by age (i.e., 12 to &lt;18 years and adult patients aged 18 years or older) during randomization was removed. Review comment: In study PRN1008-018, patients with primary ITP ages \ge 12 will be enrolled. The Agency recommends that stratification should also include age (patients aged 12 to &lt;18 years and adult patients aged 18 years or older) in addition to stratifying by splenectomy status (yes/no), and by severity of thrombocytopenia (platelet counts &lt;15,000/\mu\text{L} or \ge 15,000/\mu\text{L}).
- To continue on study treatment at the Week 13, the definitions of responder and nonresponder were revised to: At the end of 12 weeks of treatment, patients will be assessed for achieving a platelet response defined as platelet count of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} or a platelet count between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt;50,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline at any time during the first 12 weeks and in the absence of rescue medication in the 4 weeks prior to the latest elevated platelet count that meets platelet response criteria. Baseline is defined as the average of all the patient's Predose platelet counts (Screening and Study Day 1).Patients who respond will continue the blinded treatment period for a total of 24 weeks before entering the open-label Period.
- Patients who do not respond (including patients who receive rescue medication after Week 8) will be allowed to discontinue from the study or may enter the 28-week open-label period at the end of Week 12, receiving treatment with 400 mg BID of rilzabrutinib. Initial study medication assignment will remain blinded. After completing the Open-Label Period, patients who demonstrate a platelet response defined as platelet counts \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} or \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline at \ge 50\% of the visits without receiving rescue therapy during the last 8 weeks of the open-label period, will be allowed to enter the LTE."
- The planned number of adult participants was updated to 194 (from 153). Sample size was recalculated based on increasing the study power from 80% to 86% to detect a 20% difference in response rates between the 2 study arms.
L1610: ## Amended Clinical Trial Protocol 03 (August 11, 2022)
L1611: The primary reasons for this amendment to Amended Clinical Trial Protocol 02:
L1612: - To modify the primary endpoint and objective to specify participants having platelet counts at or above 50,000/\mu\text{L} for \ge two-thirds of at least 8 nonmissing weekly scheduled platelet measurements during the last 12 weeks of the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy, provided that at least 2 nonmissing weekly scheduled platelet
L1613: measurements are at or above 50,000/\muL during the last 6 weeks of the 24-week blinded treatment period. This change does not apply to the EU (EEA countries) and the UK.
L1614: - To require conducting platelet count testing at local laboratories.
- To provide guidelines for temporary discontinuation of study medication.
- To move the IBLs assessment from the Secondary to the Exploratory endpoints (not applicable for the EU [EEA countries] and UK).
- To introduce a fatigue assessment using Item 10 of the ITP-PAQ, as a Key Secondary endpoint.
- To provide new instructions for the long-term extension duration and study visit schedule.
- To add efficacy objectives and endpoints to the open-label and long-term extension periods.
L1615: ## Amended Clinical Trial Protocol 04 (July 24, 2023)
L1616: Amendment 4 was implemented July 24, 2023. The primary reasons for this amendment and main changes to the protocol included the following:
L1617: - Update the 2-sided significance level from 0.01 to 0.05 for the hypothesis testing of the primary efficacy end point.
- Clarify distinct durations of participation in the LTE for the adult and pediatric populations.
- Updated the 2-sided significance level from 0.01 to 0.05 for the hypothesis testing (i.e., Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel test) of the primary efficacy end point.
- Updated the text on calculating baseline platelet count and added a footnote for calculating baseline platelet count based on availability of 3 platelet counts, or two platelet counts when the second qualifying count is collected on Study Day 1 prior to first dose.
- Exclusion of participants with known allergy to any of the study medication, their analogues, or excipients.
- The word “must” was added to the following sentence to ensure safety and comply with regulatory guidance. “Any female participant who becomes pregnant while participating in the study must discontinue study intervention or be withdrawn from the study.”
L1618: ## 15.2. LUNA 2
L1619: ## 15.2.1. Study Design
L1620: **Study Title:** An Adaptive, Open-Label, Dose-Finding, Phase 1/2 Study Investigating the Safety, Pharmacokinetics, and Clinical Activity of Rilzabrutinib (PRN1008), an Oral BTK Inhibitor, in Patients with Relapsed Immune Thrombocytopenia
L1621: LUNA 2 was a global, 2-part, multicenter, open-label, dose-finding (Part A), with cohort expansion (Part B) safety, pharmacokinetics, and activity estimating study of rilzabrutinib in participants with primary or secondary ITP.
L1622: Part A enrolled participants with refractory or relapsed ITP with no available and approved therapeutic options and who had a platelet count &lt;30,000/\mu\text{L} and evaluated starting doses ranging from 200 mg to 400 mg po BID. Part B was an open-label study of rilzabrutinib in participants with ITP of at least 3 months duration who had had a response to IVIg/anti-D or corticosteroids that was not sustained and failed at least one other ITP therapy (that was not IVIg or corticosteroids) who had relapsed or had had an insufficient response to prior therapies and who had a platelet count &lt;30,000/\mu\text{L} and treated all participants at the selected dosage for cohort expansion of 400 mg orally twice daily. Efficacy was based on a 24-week treatment period.
L1623: In Part A, the primary efficacy endpoint was participants proportion of patients able to achieve two or more consecutive platelet counts, separated by at least 5 days, of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} AND an increase of platelet count of \ge 20,000/\mu\text{L} from baseline, by dose level, without use of rescue medication in the 4 weeks prior to the latest elevated platelet count.
L1624: In Part B, the primary efficacy endpoint was durable platelet response, defined as the proportion of patients who were able to achieve platelet counts \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} on at least 8 out of the last 12 weeks of the 24-week treatment period without the use of rescue medication after 10 weeks of active treatment.
L1625: ## 16. Efficacy
L1626: ## 16.1. LUNA 3
L1627: ## 16.1.1. Summary of Statistical Analysis Plan, Version 3
L1628: ## Analysis Population
L1629: The following analysis sets were defined for the statistical analysis:
L1630: - Intent-to-Treat (ITT) Population: the ITT population
- Modified ITT (mITT) Population: the mITT population included all participants from ITT population who are exposed. Participants were analyzed according to the intervention allocated by randomization. The mITT Population was used for sensitivity analysis.
- Safety Population: All randomized participants took at least 1 dose of study intervention, regardless of the amount of treatment administrated.
L1631: ## Analysis of the Primary Efficacy Endpoint
L1632: The primary efficacy endpoint was durable platelet response. The primary efficacy analysis was the Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel test adjusted by randomization stratification factors based on the ITT population. Stratification factors include the randomization strata of splenectomy status (yes, no) and severity of thrombocytopenia (platelet counts &lt;15,000/\mu\text{L}, \ge 15,000/\mu\text{L}). The Mantel-Haenszel estimate of common risk difference in response rates and its associated 95% CI based on Mantel-Haenszel stratum weights and the Sato variance estimator were reported. The
L1633: observed response rate for each treatment group was presented along with its associated 95% asymptotic CI.
L1634: ## **Analysis of the Key Secondary Efficacy Endpoints**
L1635: The primary efficacy analysis was based on log-rank test adjusted by randomization stratification factors. Hazard ratio and its 95% CI were modeled using Cox regression model with treatment and randomization stratification factors as covariates. The fifth key secondary endpoint was the change from baseline on Item 10 of the ITP-PAQ (i.e., physical fatigue) in adult participants (\ge 18 years) at Week 13. The primary efficacy analysis was based on the ANCOVA model with the treatment group, randomization stratification factors, and geographic region as fixed effects and baseline Item 10 score as a covariate.
L1636: ## **Number of Weeks With a Platelet Response**
L1637: For the first and second key secondary endpoints, number of weeks with platelet response, the primary efficacy analyses were based on the MMRM approach was used to analyze the longitudinal binary platelet response data across visits in the ITT population. The analysis model included treatment, randomization stratification factors, week (Weeks 2 to 25), treatment-by-week interaction as categorical fix effects. Generalized estimating equations (GEE) was used to obtain statistical inference. A variance component working covariance matrix was used in the model to obtain unweighted proportions. Least squares (LS) mean number of weeks (% weeks) with platelet response across 24 weeks in each treatment group, difference in LS mean number of weeks (% weeks) with platelet response between treatment groups and its corresponding 95% CIs were provided along with the p-value. Number of weeks with platelet counts \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and at least doubled from baseline over the 24-week blinded treatment period in the absence of rescue therapy
L1638: Time to first platelet count of \ge 50,000/\mu\text{L} OR between \ge 30,000/\mu\text{L} and &lt; 50,000/\mu\text{L} and doubled from baseline
L1639: ## **Time to First Platelet Response**
L1640: The third key secondary endpoint was time to first platelet response. The primary efficacy analysis was based on log-rank test adjusted by randomization stratification factors. Hazard ratio and its 95% CI were modeled using Cox regression model with treatment and randomization stratification factors as covariates. Platelet counts were considered as no response during the use of rescue therapy and for 4 weeks after the rescue therapy ended.
L1641: ## **Proportion of Participants Requiring Rescue Therapy During the 24-Week Blinded Treatment Period**
L1642: The fourth key secondary endpoint was proportion of participants requiring rescue therapy. The primary efficacy analysis was based on log-rank test adjusted by randomization stratification factors. Hazard ratio and its 95% CI were modeled using Cox regression model with treatment and randomization stratification factors as covariates.
L1643: ## Change From Baseline on Item 10 of the ITP-PAQ (i.e., Physical Fatigue) in Adult Participants (\ge 18 Years) at Week 13
L1644: The fifth key secondary endpoint was the change from baseline on Item 10 of the ITP-PAQ (i.e., physical fatigue) in adult participants (\ge 18 years) at Week 13. The primary efficacy analysis was based on the ANCOVA model with the treatment group, randomization stratification factors, and geographic region as fixed effects and baseline Item 10 score as a covariate. Missing score at Week 13 was imputed via worst observation carried forward (WOCF) for the participants who were rescued after 8 weeks of double-blind treatment or discontinued before Week 13 due to lack of efficacy or related adverse events, or via multiple imputation otherwise. Each complete dataset was analyzed by fitting an ANCOVA model. Statistical inference obtained from all imputed data were combined using Rubin's rule. LS mean score changes in each treatment group and that between treatment groups as well as their corresponding 95% CIs were provided along with the p-value.
L1645: ## **Multiple Testing Strategy**
L1646: To control the family-wise type I error, a fixed-sequence hierarchical testing procedure was applied to the primary endpoint and key secondary endpoints at 2-sided 5% significant level i.e., each hypothesis was formally tested only if the preceding one was statistically significant. The hierarchical testing order was prespecified and is presented in the Section 6.2.1.2.
L1647: ## **Handling of Missing Data**
L1648: The primary analysis for handling discontinuation of study intervention before Week 25 due to lack of response or related AE participants were considered as nonresponder.
L1649: Two sensitivity analyses (SA) were conducted to further address the impact of missing data not due to related Adverse Events or lack of response to demonstrate the robustness of the primary analysis for the primary endpoint (Table 115).
L1650: **Table 115. Sensitivity Analyses for Primary Endpoint, LUNA 3**
L1651: SA	Descriptive
SA 1	Tipping point analysis; missing considered due to missing data, or discontinuation not due to related adverse events or lack of response
SA 2	missing platelet count due to COVID-19 imputed using the participant's median value of available weekly platelet counts
L1652: Source: FDA reviewer summary
L1653: Abbreviations: COVID-19, coronavirus disease of 2019; SA, sensitivity analysis
L1654: ## 16.1.2. Sensitivity Analyses of Durable Platelet Response During 12-Week and 24-Week Double-Blind Period
L1655: Sensitivity analyses were conducted to handle all types of missing data, regardless of the reasons for missing data or dropouts, rather than for only treated lack of response or related AE as nonresponder. Table 116 and Table 117 present tipping point analyses for the 12-week and 24-week periods, respectively. In these analyses, participants with missing response data were imputed as responders at varying probabilities—denoted as p_0 for the placebo group and p_1 for
L1656: the rilzabrutinib group. The values of p_0 and p_1 were incrementally increased from 0% to 100% in 10% steps to evaluate the impact of different imputation assumptions on the treatment effect. Table 116 and Table 117 showed that the statistical significance of the treatment effect was lost when the assumed response rate among missing participants is relatively high in the placebo group (p_0 close to 0.2) and relatively low in the rilzabrutinib group (p_1 close to 0). For example, when p_0=0.2, the p-value is no longer significant if p_1=0 or 0.1. In this study, the placebo group had a durable response rate below 5%, indicating that assuming a higher p_0 is not consistent with the observed data. Overall, the tipping point results demonstrated that the primary analyses results for rilzabrutinib on durable platelet response during the 12-week and 24-week double-blind period remain robust (see Section 6.2.1.4.3

L1660: Given a set of (p_0, p_1) where p_0 and p_1 are the response rate among those with missing durable response status in the placebo group and the rilzabrutinib group respectively, a participant with missing response will be randomly assigned as a responder or a nonresponder using binomial distribution to generate multiple imputed datasets. Each imputed complete dataset is analyzed using Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel test. The Chi-square statistic is then normalized via Wilson-Hilferty transformation. And the results across multiple complete datasets are combined using Rubin's rule

L1674: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L1675: ## 17.1. Safety Analysis Populations, Definitions
L1676: The following pooled populations were used in one or more safety analyses.
L1677: **Primary Safety Set:** Used to establish primary safety profile of rilzabrutinib in ITP. Comprises 133 participants who received rilzabrutinib 400 mg BID and 65 participants who received placebo during the double-blind treatment period on LUNA 3.
L1678: **Integrated Safety Population (ISP):** Used to confirm the primary safety profile and to identify rare but serious events that might only be observed in a larger population. Comprises 276 participants with chronic ITP who received at least one dose of rilzabrutinib at the proposed dosage (400 mg BID) on LUNA 3 or LUNA 2. This includes 133 participants randomized to receive rilzabrutinib during the DB on LUNA 3, 65 participants who received placebo during the DB period and went on to receive rilzabrutinib during the OL period(s) and 52 participants from PRN1008-10 Part A and 26 from Part B. The analysis includes data from DB, OL, and LTE periods.
L1679: **ITP Rilzabrutinib Pool:** The Applicant defined pooled population comprising all ITP adult participants exposed to rilzabrutinib during the entire treatment period (Main/DB, OL, and LTE periods, cumulatively) (LUNA 3 and LUNA 2) (N=284 rilzabrutinib any dose). This pool is used when discussing adverse events of special interest and AEs of clinical interest in Section 7.6.3.

L1777: ## **Hemoglobin**
L1778: The mean change from baseline over time for hemoglobin values for rilzabrutinib and placebo treated participants is shown in Figure 42. At Week 13, the changes from baseline appear to diverge. This is likely due to response in the rilzabrutinib treated participants who remained on study in the DB treatment period and those treated with placebo who were permitted withdrawal due to nonresponse.

L1860: A clinical inspection summary was completed by the Division of Clinical Compliance Evaluation in the Office of Scientific Investigations with a signature date of May 27, 2025, with amended signature on July 31, 2025. Three clinical investigator inspections were completed for the study: Lei Zhang, M.D., Howard Liebman, M.D., and Wojciech Homenda, M.D. An inspection of the Contract Research Organization, [REDACTED](b) (4), was also completed. Based on the results of the inspections, the study data derived from the above inspected clinical investigator sites are considered acceptable. The Contract Research Organization's monitoring for the study appears adequate. In general, the clinical data submitted to the Agency for assessment appear to be acceptable in support of the proposed indication.

L1869: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1870: ## PMR 1
L1871: Conduct a clinical drug interaction study to evaluate the effect of WAYRILZ (rilzabrutinib) on the pharmacokinetics of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 transporter substrates in adult healthy volunteers. The study will assess the magnitude of exposure change of the transporter substrates and inform appropriate drug interaction management strategy for coadministration of rilzabrutinib with these transporter substrates in adult patients with persistent or chronic immune thrombocytopenia. This study should be designed and conducted in accordance with the ICH guidance for industry *M12 Drug Interaction Studies* (August 2024).
L1872: ## PMR 2
L1873: Conduct a dedicated renal impairment study to evaluate the effect of severe renal impairment (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min) on WAYRILZ (rilzabrutinib) pharmacokinetics compared to subjects with normal renal function. The study will assess the magnitude of exposure change of rilzabrutinib and inform appropriate dosing instructions for patients with severe renal impairment. This study should be designed and conducted in accordance with guidance for industry *Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Renal Function — Study Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing* (July 2024).
L1874: ## PMR 3
L1875: Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to WAYRILZ (rilzabrutinib) during pregnancy to assess the risk of pregnancy and maternal complications and adverse effects on the developing fetus, neonate, and infant. Assess infant outcomes through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.
L1876: ## PMR 4
L1877: Perform a milk-only lactation study in lactating women who have received rilzabrutinib to measure concentrations of WAYRILZ (rilzabrutinib) in breast milk using a validated assay. Assess the effects on the breastfed infant, if available, based on study population.

L1971: ## 1. Summary
L1972: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of PRN1008, when administered via oral gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice.
L1973: ## Rat Study:
L1974: ## Survival data analyses:
L1975: - For male rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and the treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups.
- For female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and the treated groups. However, p-value of the likelihood ratio test is 0.0295 &lt; 0.05 but the p-value of log-rank test is 0.1518 &gt; 0.05. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups.
L1976: ## Tumor data analysis:
L1977: - For female rats, the tumor data analysis showed statistically significant positive dose response relationships across the vehicle control and the treated groups in the incidence of adenoma, adrenocortical benign, gland adrenal (P-value=0.0139 &lt; 0.025).
- For female rats, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the medium dose group showed statistically significant increases in the incidence of carcinoma, follicular cell, malignant, gland thyroid (P-value=0.0076 &lt; 0.01).
L1978: ## Mouse Study:
L1979: ## Survival data analyses:
L1980: - The survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and the treated groups in
L1981: either male or female mice. The pairwise comparisons did not show statistically significant difference in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups in either male or female mice.
L1982: 2. The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increase in mortality in the positive control group than the vehicle control group male mice.
L1983: ## Tumor data analysis:
L1984: - For male mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the positive control showed statistically significant increases in the incidences of adenoma, benign, Lung (p-value&lt;0.001) and carcinoma, malignant, Lung (p-value=0.0011 &lt;0.05), hemangiosarcoma, malignant, Spleen (p-value&lt;0.001), and combined tumors of hemangiosarcoma, whole body (p-value&lt;0.001).
- For female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the positive control showed statistically significant increases in the incidences of adenoma, benign, Lung (p-value&lt;0.001) and carcinoma, malignant, Lung (p-value=&lt;0.001), hemangiosarcoma, malignant, Spleen (p-value&lt;0.001), and combined tumors of hemangiosarcoma, whole body (p-value&lt;0.001).
L1985: ## 2. Background
L1986: In this submission the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in rats and one in mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of PRN1008, when administered via oral gavage at appropriate drug levels for 104 weeks in rats and 26 weeks in mice.
L1987: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as the dose increases.
L1988: ## 3. Rat Study
L1989: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and forty rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the four groups in equal size of 60 rats per group. The dose levels for treated groups were 10, 30 and 100 mg/kg/day for males and 5, 15 and 50 mg/kg/day for females. The study for the rats was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks.
L1990: Table 1: Study Design in Rat Study
L1991: Protocol Group No.	Dose Levels (mg/kg/day)	Identification	Number of Animals Enrolled
Males	Females
1	0	Vehicle	60	60
2	10/5	Low	60	60
3	30/15	Mid	60	60
4	100/50	High	60	60
L1992: ## 3.1. Sponsor's analyses
L1993: ## 3.1.1. Survival analysis
L1994: Kaplan-Meier estimates of group survival rates were calculated and shown graphically. A log-rank dose response trend test of survival rates was performed utilizing ordinal coefficients. In addition, a log-rank test for survival was used to make pair-wise comparisons of each treated group with the control group. Both the trend test and pair-wise comparisons were conducted
L1995: at the 0.05 significance level. Survival times in which the status of the animal's death is classified as an accidental death or terminal sacrifice were considered censored values for the purpose of the Kaplan-Meier estimates and survival rate analyses.
L1996: Sponsor's findings: Sponsor's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of survival as 38 (63%), 46 (77%), 43 (72%), and 34 (57%) in vehicle, low, mid, high dose groups, respectively in males and 37 (62%), 35 (58%), 34 (57%), and 27 (45%) in vehicle, low, mid, high dose groups, respectively in females.
L1997: There were no statistically significant findings in survival rates when comparing active treatment groups with the control group
L1998: ## 3.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L1999: The incidence of tumors was analyzed by Peto's mortality-prevalence method, without continuity correction, incorporating the context (incidental, fatal, or mortality independent) in which tumors were observed. The following fixed intervals were used for incidental tumor analyses: weeks 1-50, 51-80, 81-end of study (up to the initiation of terminal sacrifice), and scheduled terminal sacrifice.
L2000: Tumors classified as mortality-independent were analyzed with Peto's mortality-independent method incorporating the day of detection. Each diagnosed tumor type was analyzed separately. All metastases and invasive tumors were considered secondary and not included in the statistical analysis.
L2001: Each tumor type was analyzed with a 1-sided trend test using ordinal coefficients. In addition, pair-wise comparisons with the control group were conducted for each active treatment group. An exact permutation test was conducted for analyses with low tumor incidence.
L2002: Statistical significance was determined according to the following guidelines: trend tests were conducted at the 0.01 and 0.05 significance levels for common and rare tumors,
L2003: respectively. Pair-wise comparisons with the control group were conducted at the 0.05 significance level for both common and rare tumors. A rare tumor was defined as one in which the spontaneous tumor rate was less than 1%. The spontaneous tumor rate was determined based on the concurrent control groups and historical control data of the conducting laboratory and animal supplier.
L2004: ## Sponsor's findings:
L2005: - For males, the tumor incidence of the granular cell tumor, benign in the brain was 0, 0, 4 and 2 for the vehicle, 10, 30 and 100 mg/kg/day treatment groups, respectively. The tumor was considered rare and the pairwise comparison of the 30 mg/kg/day group with the vehicle group was statistically significant (pvalue = 0.0498). In addition, the trend test across dose groups was statistically significant (p-value = 0.0363). No other pairwise comparisons were statistically significant.
- For females, the tumor incidence of the adenoma, adrenocortical, benign in the adrenal gland was 0, 0, 1 and 3 for the vehicle, 5, 15 and 50 mg/kg/day treatment groups, respectively. The tumor was considered rare and the trend test across groups was statistically significant (p-value = 0.0339). None of the pairwise comparisons were statistically significant.
L2006: ## 3.2. Reviewer's analyses
L2007: To verify sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.
L2008: ## 3.2.1. Survival analysis
L2009: The survival distributions of animals in four groups were estimated by the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship and homogeneity of survival distributions were tested for the, vehicle control, low, medium and high dose groups using the Likelihood Ratio test and the Log-Rank test. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 7 and 8 in the appendix for males and females, respectively. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rate are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for males and females, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 9 to 10 in the appendix for males and females, respectively.
L2010: Reviewer's findings: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of survival were 31 (52%), 37 (62%), 35 (58%), and 31 (52%) in vehicle, and low, mid, high dose groups, respectively in males and 37 (62%), 32 (53%), 29 (48%), and 24 (40%) in vehicle, and low, mid, high dose groups, respectively in females.
L2011: Note that there is a discrepancy between the reviewer's survival rate and the sponsor's survival rate. The reason is that there were missing rats in the sponsor's data. The total number of rats in each of the groups were 52, 51, 51, 56 for males and 58, 56, 54, 53 for females.
L2012: For male rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and the treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups.
L2013: For female rats, the survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and the treated groups. However, p-value of the likelihood ratio test is 0.0295 &lt; 0.05 but the p-value of log-rank test is 0.1518 &gt; 0.05. We consider the test results as a positive finding when both p-values are less than 0.05. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality between the vehicle control group and any of the treated groups.
L2014: ## 3.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L2015: The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the pairwise comparison between each of the treated groups with control group. Both the dose response relationship tests and the pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-K method, described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period (W_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of S_h = 1. An animal that dies at week W_h without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of S_h = \left(\frac{W_h}{W_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted
L2016: group size is defined as \sum S_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score S_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score S_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum S_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used. The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons are listed in Tables 11 and 12 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2017: Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study and one transgenic mouse study, the more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.005 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.025 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisons for the chronic rat study in the above type of submission with one chronic rat study and one transgenic mouse study, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.01 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study.
L2018: It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008) showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for Poly-K tests.
L2019: Reviewer's findings: Tables 2 and 3 below show the p-values less than or equal to 0.05 for the positive dose response and the pairwise comparisons between vehicle control and treated groups for males.
L2020: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed in the data analysis section, we make the following statistical conclusions:
L2021: - For female rats, the tumor data analysis showed statistically significant positive dose response relationships across the vehicle control and the treated groups in the incidences of adenoma, adrenocortical benign, gland adrenal (P-value=0.0139&lt;0.025).
- For female rats, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the medium dose group showed statistically significant increases in incidences of carcinoma, follicular cell, malignant, gland thyroid (P-value=0.0076&lt;0.01).

L2027: ## 4. Mouse Study
L2028: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, one vehicle control group and one positive control group. One hundred and ten mice of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated and control groups. There were 25 mice in each of the vehicle control group and treated groups. The dose levels for treated groups were 30, 100, and 300 mg/kg/day for both males and females. There were ten mice in positive control group for each sex. Animals in positive control group were administered urethane on Days 1, 3, and 5

L2031: ## 4.1. Sponsor's analyses
L2032: ## 4.1.1. Survival analysis
L2033: Kaplan-Meier estimates of the group survival rates were calculated and shown graphically.
L2034: For Groups 1-4, a log-rank test for survival was used to compare the homogeneity of survival rates across the groups. If the survival rates are significantly different, a log-rank test was used to make pairwise comparisons of each treated group with the vehicle control group. In addition, Group 5 (positive control) was compared to the vehicle control group with a log-rank test. All tests were conducted at the 0.05 significance level.
L2035: Survival times in which the status of the animal's death is classified as an accidental death or terminal euthanasia were considered censored values for the purpose of the Kaplan-Meier estimates and survival rate analyses.
L2036: Sponsor's findings: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival were 25 (100%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), 25 (100%), and 5 (50%) in male mice, and 22 (88%), 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 23 (92%) and 9 (90%) in female mice in vehicle control, low, medium, high and positive control, respectively.
L2037: The sponsor concluded that, there were no statistically findings in survival rates when comparing test article groups with the vehicle control group. Among males, the survival rate in the positive control group was significantly lower when compared with the vehicle control group.
L2038: ## 4.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L2039: For Groups 1-4, the incidence of tumors was analyzed by Peto's mortality prevalence method, without continuity correction, and incorporating the context (incidental, fatal, or mortality-independent) in which tumors were observed. Among males, one 30/mg/kg/day and one 100 mg/kg/day animal did not complete the study through the scheduled terminal sacrifice. Among females, three vehicle control, one 30/mg/kg/day and two 300 mg/kg/day animals did not complete the study through the scheduled terminal sacrifice. Because so few animals died during the study and, with the exception of one female vehicle control animal that died on day 55 with a multicentric lymphoma, all animals were at risk for at least 134 days, the fixed interval used for incidental tumor analysis was Days 1-end of study including scheduled terminal sacrifice. All tumors in the scheduled terminal sacrifice interval were considered incidental for the purpose of statistical analysis.
L2040: Tumors classified as mortality-independent were analyzed with Peto's mortality independent method incorporating the day of detection.
L2041: Each tumor type was analyzed with a 1-sided trend test using ordinal coefficients. In addition, pairwise comparisons with the vehicle control group were conducted for each active treatment group. An exact permutation test was conducted for analyses with low tumor incidence.
L2042: Incidence of tumors in the positive control group (Group 5) were compared to vehicle control group (Group 1) with Fisher's exact test. All tests were conducted at the 0.05 significance level.
L2043: Sponsor's findings: The sponsor concluded that there were no statistically significant tumor findings in the test article groups when compared to the vehicle control group.
L2044: There was a statistically significant increase in the following tumors when comparing the positive control group with the vehicle control group:

L2131: Diagram illustrating the mechanism of action of Rilzabrutinib in B cells and macrophages. In B cells, Rilzabrutinib inhibits BTK activation downstream of the B-cell receptor + antigen binding, blocking Anti-platelet antibody production. In macrophages, Rilzabrutinib inhibits BTK activation downstream of Fc gamma receptor + anti-platelet antibody binding, blocking Phagocytosis.

L2136: Diagram illustrating the Study Design, LUNA 3.
The study begins with a 28-day Screen period, followed by 2:1 Randomization into two arms: Rilzabrutinib \pm SOC and Placebo \pm SOC.
The Blinded Treatment Period runs from Week 1 to Week 12. During this period, participants receive 400 mg BID \pm SOC (Optional SOC = TPO-RA or CS). The PLT Count Assessment occurs during this period.
At Week 13, participants are assessed for response. Non-responders can join the Open-Label Period after Week 12. Responders join the Open-Label Period after Week 24.
The Open-Label Period runs from Week 13 to Week 53. The Primary Endpoint is assessed during this period.
Week 25 is marked as the last visit of the blinded treatment period and the start of the open-label period.
Following the Open-Label Period, there is a Long-Term Extension phase, which includes a 4-week follow-up period, leading to the Last Day of Study Drug and End of Study.
Abbreviations: BID = twice daily; CS = corticosteroid; PLT = platelet; SOC = standard of care; TPO-RA = thrombopoietin receptor agonist.
L2137: Figure 7: Plot of Median Platelet Count by Visit During the 24-Week Double-Blind Treatment Period, LUNA 3. The graph shows median platelet count (10^9/L) on the Y-axis (0 to 80) versus Double-blind period (Week) on the X-axis (Weeks 2 through 25). Two groups are compared: Placebo (N=69, dashed line with triangles) and Rilzabrutinib 400 mg BID (N=133, solid line with circles). The Rilzabrutinib group consistently shows higher median platelet counts than the Placebo group across all visits. The Rilzabrutinib group starts around 20 x 10^9/L at Week 2 and rises sharply to around 65 x 10^9/L by Week 15, remaining high thereafter. The Placebo group starts around 15 x 10^9/L at Week 2 and remains relatively stable around 20-30 x 10^9/L throughout the study period. Below the X-axis, the number of participants (N) for each visit is listed for both groups.

L2154: ADME data:
Physico-chemical parameters and plasma protein binding: measured
Absorption, ADAM model: prediction of F_a (based on SIVA modeling)
Distribution: prediction of V_{ss} using Full PBPK model (Study 015)
Metabolism: In vitro reaction phenotyping
Elimination: measured CL_{iv} and CL_r used in reverse translational tool (Study 015)
Interaction: in vitro CYP inhibition in HLM and CYP induction in human hepatocytes
The flowchart illustrates the Modeling and Simulation Strategy:
Building of initial PBPK model (Influenced by ADME data and In vivo DDI data for qualification - control arms (Studies 008 and 014)).
Optimization of K_i CYP3A4 and Optimization of f_m CYP3A4 (Influenced by In vivo DDI data for refinement: interaction of rilabrutinib - with midazolam (Study 011) - with ritonavir (Study 014)). This step also involves Manual optimization with help of sensitivity analysis.
One final PBPK model (Influenced by In vivo DDI data for external validation: interaction of rilabrutinib - with rifampicin (Study 024)).
Predict DDI potential using the final PBPK model: - Effect of weak and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors/inducers on rilabrutinib
